Let's explore inversion-based algorithm in detail.

Given current x (sum=1). Compute c=conv(x,x) length 2n-1. We want modify c to reduce max and flatten. We can define a target t of length at least n (first n) from c using an operation:
- Option A: exponential transform: t_k = exp((c_k - μ)/τ) maybe then scale sum of t? But x inversion only uses first n. To keep sum x=1, after inversion scale x. If t is flat, x from square-root gives sum not 1; scaling changes c by factor. But if we scale t to ensure sum(t)=? Need relation: If t is target first n conv of y, then y inversion may yield sum S_y not known. After scaling y to sum1, first n conv scales by 1/S_y^2. To make them match a desired level L, we need choose t such that L = inv_sum(t)^? Hard. But we can simply compute y=invert(t), normalize to sum1, evaluate; iteration doesn't need exact fixed point.

- Option B: Directly compute y by solving recurrence from t = (1-η)c[:n] + η*L (where L=1/(2n-1) maybe). Then normalize y, combine with x. This is like alternating projection between x-space and c-space. Could use relaxation η to ensure stability.

But recurrence requires t[0]>0. c[0]=x0^2. If c[0] becomes too small, x0 small and inversion unstable. Keep t[0] > threshold.

Could also use full c (length 2n-1) and inversion using symmetric? For k≥n, cannot recursively determine because x length already determined; they are consistency. But we can use them as loss. Could use full c in gradient.

Maybe use "alternating minimization over c and x": For fixed c target, minimize ||x*x - c||^2. The gradient descent for x given c is grad = 4 conv(c - x*x, x) (since derivative of ||c - x*x||^2 = -4 (c-x*x)*x? sign). We can do steepest descent to match a chosen target c. Let's define projected simplex function:
Given v, find x_i ≥0, sum=1 minimizing ||v - x||_2. Standard:
sort v descending, find ρ = max{u: v_u - (sum_{j≤u} v_j -1)/u >0}; τ=(sum_{j≤u} v_j -1)/u; x=max(v-τ,0).
Implement O(n log n). For many iterations (100k) O(n log n) ~1024*10=10k, 1e9 maybe too slow in Python? Need faster. Since n fixed and x positive interior mostly, we can just clip negatives and renormalize: x = np.clip(v,0,None); S=x.sum(); if S<=0 reset; x/=S. This is not exact projection but common; small clipping rare. Faster O(n). We can use this. It may bias but okay. For exact projection optional periodically.

Need center gradient to maintain sum approximately: grad_sub_mean = grad - grad.mean(). Then x + lr*direction stays sum 1 before clip. Good.

Since objective smooth (except true max), we can use projected Adam with adaptive learning rate based on true score improvement.

Pseudo:
```
def adam_optimize(x, deadline, p=4.0, use_l2=True, l2_weight=0.5, T=None, lr=0.01, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, eps=1e-8, check_every=1, verbose_prefix=""):
    x = x.copy(); x = clip_renormalize(x)
    m = zeros; v = zeros; t=0; lr_base=lr; best_x=x; best_score=score(x)
    no_improve=0
    while time < deadline:
        conv = compute_conv_fft(x)
        # choose gradient
        if p=2: weights = conv - mean
        elif p>2: weights = conv**(p-1)
        g=grad_power(conv, x, p)
        if l2: g += l2_weight*grad_l2
        # center gradient
        g -= g.mean()
        m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
        v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*g*g
        m_hat = m/(1-beta1**t); v_hat = v/(1-beta2**t)
        step = lr * m_hat / (sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
        # Maybe scale step by norm? Projected gradient with lr on z; Adam step magnitude per coordinate ~lr. But x entries ~1/n or singular ~1/√n; step 0.01 too large. Need scale.
```
We need choose learning rate carefully. x values ~0.01-0.03; gradient magnitude? Let's estimate. conv weights ~1/n. grad = 2 conv(weights, x). For p=2 weights ~1/n, conv weights*x ~? sum over O(n) (1/n)*(1/√n)? Wait x ~1/√n for square-root? Actually normalized x ~1/√n at high index? x0~0.028, x_n~0.0005, average ~0.001? sum1 -> average 0.001. Weights ~0.0007; conv of length ~n: n*0.0007*0.001/√? ~? Let's approximate gradient components O(1/√n)? Maybe 0.01. Adam normalizes per-coordinate; step lr=0.01 may be okay but too large? Project to simplex after step may change many. Need adapt.

Pseudo `momentum_optimize`:
```
x = normalize
best_x=x; best_score=true_score(x)
d = zeros; mu=0.85
alpha=0.002
min_alpha=1e-6; max_alpha=0.05
no_improve=0
while time < deadline:
   c=conv_fft(x)
   # gradient combination
   g = combine grad (centered)
   gn=norm(g)
   if gn<1e-12 break
   dir = -g/gn
   d = mu*d + (1-mu)*dir
   dn=norm(d)
   if dn>0: d = d/dn # keep direction unit l2
   # optionally oscillation check if dot(old_dir, new_dir)<0
   for attempt in range(10):
       x_trial = clip_renormalize(x + alpha*d)
       s = score_fast(x_trial)
       if s < best_score - 1e-12:
           accept
           break
       alpha *= 0.5
   if accepted:
       no_improve=0; alpha = min(max_alpha, alpha*1.1)
       if s < best_score: update best
       if alpha < min_alpha*? break
   else:
       no_improve+=1
       mu *=0.9
       if no_improve>100: break
```
This is essentially gradient descent with adaptive step. However using normalized gradient and momentum may get stuck due to flat directions? Could use Adam better. But this is stable.

But row max 2n-2, col min0, difference up to 2n-2, but only if f index =d valid up to n-1; for d>n-1 no. So offset d (row-col positive) means subdiagonal? In sparse matrix, diagonal offset 0 col, offset d row=col+d (below main). Values f[d]. Thus A = sum_{d=0}^{n-1} sp.diags([f[d]*ones(n-d)], offsets=d, shape=(2n-1,n)). This can be built vectorized quickly. Good. Then linprog. But `sp.diags` with list of arrays maybe O(n^2) but C. For n=1024, 1M entries fine. For n=2048, 8M. We can build each LP. Use `method='highs'`. Might be okay for few.

But is LP direction efficient? Let's test concept: Given f normalized sum1, current max M. LP max sum(g) s.t. f*g <= M, g>=0. The solution g may have sum >1. We rescale to sum1, blend. This is like finding a point in simplex satisfying linearized stability. Could produce large improvements. We can call it occasionally.

Structured initializations:
- Uniform: ones/n.
- SqrtFlat: compute from recurrence with target flat first n. Implementation:
```
def sqrt_flat_sequence(n):
    alpha = 1.0 / (2*n - 1)
    x = np.zeros(n)
    x[0] = np.sqrt(alpha)
    for k in range(1,n):
       if k==1: s=0
       else: s = np.dot(x[1:k], x[k-1:0:-1])  # careful x[1:k] length k-1, reversed x[1:k]
       x[k] = (alpha - s)/(2*x[0])
       if x[k] < 0: x[k]=0.0
    return normalize_sum(x,1.0)
```
Check reversed slice: for k=3, x[1:3]=[x1,x2], reversed [x2,x1], dot gives x1*x2+x2*x1 correct. For k=1, slice empty.
But this recurrence assumes target t[0]=alpha. If we clip negatives, sum may change; normalize.

- Generalized power seq: `x_i = (i + a + 1)**(-b)` normalized. Need choose a=0.5, b=0.5 maybe; a=1, b=0.25, etc. We'll make list of params.

- Parent interpolated to n: if parent available and length different, use `np.interp`.

- Random smooth: generate Fourier sine? Maybe `x = 1 + 0.5*np.sin(2π freq/n + phase) + noise`, clip. But structured likely enough.

def first_order_optimize(x0, deadline, start_decay=0.2, end_decay=0.05, alpha0=None, label="", verbose=True):
    x = normalize_sum(x0.copy(),1.0)
    n=len(x)
    best_x=x.copy(); best_score=score_fast(x)
    t_it=0
    alpha = alpha0 or max(0.002, 0.1*np.linalg.norm(x))
    alpha_min=1e-6; alpha_max=0.1
    mu=0.8
    d=np.zeros(n)
    no_improve=0
    total_time = deadline - time.monotonic()
    elapsed_frac=-1
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       elapsed_frac = (time.monotonic()-t0?)/total? We can pass start? Use loops: maybe outer phases; inside compute phase ratio from entry_time.
```

```
       conv = conv_fft(x, fft_len)
       maxc = float(conv.max()); meanc = float(conv.sum()/(2*n-1))
       # adaptive weight: if no_improve high, increase peak focus? 
       frac = (time.monotonic()-t0_inner)/(t_end-t0_inner)
       temp_frac = 0.5 - 0.45*frac  # 0.5 ->0.05
       peak_weight = 1.0
       flat_weight = 0.5*(1-frac) + 0.05  # 0.55 ->0.05
       # maybe use power if flat_weight small? Use softmax peak and flat L2.
       g = gradient_combined(x, conv, temp_frac, peak_weight, flat_weight)
       g -= g.mean()
       gn = norm(g)
       if gn < 1e-12: break
       dir = -g/gn
       d = 0.9*d + 0.1*dir
       dn=norm(d)
       if dn>0: d = d/dn
       # if momentum direction not aligned with current dir? dot<0 maybe reset
       # line search
       improved=False
       for scale in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.015625]:
           a = alpha*scale
           if a < alpha_min: break
           x_trial = x + a*d
           x_trial = clip_renormalize(x_trial)
           s_trial = score_fast(x_trial) # uses conv each time (could expensive). We can approximate using cached? Need true.
           if s_trial < best_score - 1e-12:
               x = x_trial; best_x=x.copy(); best_score=s_trial; improved=True; alpha = min(alpha_max, a*1.2? or alpha*1.1); break
       if improved:
           no_improve=0
           if alpha < alpha_max: alpha = min(alpha_max, alpha*1.05)
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           alpha *=0.5
           d *=0.5
           if alpha < alpha_min or no_improve > 200: 
               # maybe reset momentum and try with larger flat? set alpha=0.002
               if no_improve>500: break
```

Gradient combined function:
```
def gradient_combined(x, conv, temp_frac, peak_weight, flat_weight, fft_len):
    n=len(x)
    # peak weights
    c = conv
    maxc = c.max(); meanc = c.mean()
    if maxc <= meanc: temp = 1e-12
    else: temp = temp_frac*(maxc-meanc)
    if temp <= 0: temp = 1e-12*maxc
    z = (c - maxc) / temp
    # avoid overflow: z<=0
    w_peak = np.exp(z)
    w_peak /= w_peak.sum()
    g_peak = convolve_weights_x(w_peak, x, fft_len)
    # flat weights c - meanc
    w_flat = c - meanc
    g_flat = convolve_weights_x(w_flat, x, fft_len)
    # normalize each gradient by norm
    gp = g_peak - g_peak.mean(); np=np.linalg.norm(gp)
    gf = g_flat - g_flat.mean(); nf=...
    g = peak_weight*(gp/np if np>0 else 0) + flat_weight*(gf/nf if nf>0 else 0)
    return g
```
But `convolve_weights_x` output not multiplied by 2; constant okay. Need for flat weights can be negative; FFT real okay.

The centered negative weights push low lags up. Good.

Need `clip_renormalize`: 
```
def clip_renormalize(v, target=1.0):
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, None)
    s = v.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full_like(v, target/v.size)
    return v * (target/s)
```
This may slightly break exact sum due float but okay.

`score_fast(x)`:
```
def score_fast(x):
    n=len(x)
    s=float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12: return inf
    # scale? If not sum1, compute conv and score; conv scales.
    conv = conv_fft(x)
    return float(2*n*conv.max()/(s*s))
```

`conv_fft(x)`:
```
def conv_fft(x):
    n=len(x); L=2*n-1
    f=1 << (L-1).bit_length()  # if L power? For L=1? n>=1.
    X=np.fft.rfft(x, f)
    c=np.fft.irfft(X*X, f)[:L]
    return c
```

LP function:
```
def lp_direction(f, M):
    n=len(f)
    if n > 2048: return None
    A = sp.diags(...).tocsr()
    c = -np.ones(n)
    b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, M)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)]*n
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit':?})
```
`linprog` has `options` can set time limit? HiGHS options maybe `{'time_limit': 10, 'output_flag': False}`. Use `options={"output_flag": False, "time_limit": 10}` if supported. To be safe, `options={'presolve':'default'}`? We can pass `{'time_limit': 10, 'output_flag': False}`. If unsupported? HiGHS accepts. But if method='highs'. We'll use `options={'time_limit': 5, 'output_flag': False}`.

Return result.x if success else None.

LP optimization `lp_optimize(x0, deadline, max_lp_time=20)`:
```
x=normalize; best...
step=0.2; no_improve=0
while time < deadline:
   M=score? current max conv (with sum1: conv.max())
   g=lp_direction(x, M)
   if g None: break
   g=normalize(g)
   # line search over alpha in [step, 0.5, 1?]
   for alpha in [step, 0.7*step, 0.3*step, 1.2*step, 1.5*step]:
       cand = (1-alpha)*x + alpha*g; cand=clip_renormalize
       sc = score_fast(cand)
       if sc < best: accept
   if no improve: step*=0.7; break if small
   else step *=0.98 until 0.02
```

def power_law_sequence(n, alpha, beta):
    idx = np.arange(n) + 0.5
    x = idx ** (-beta)
    # optionally taper right with factor (1 + shift*(1 - idx/n))? 
```

Let's enhance LP approach. The previous LP direction maximizes sum(g) subject to f*g ≤ M. It can be viewed as Frank-Wolfe for maximizing something. We can improve by solving with a slightly lower M (M*(1-δ)) and if infeasible, use previous. Or solve an LP with objective `max sum(g) - ε ∑ active (f*g)_k`? Linear objective with weighted active constraints? For instance, choose weights w_k for active lags and minimize ∑ w_k (f*g)_k subject to g≥0, sum=1. This is a linear program! It directly picks a direction g that minimizes a weighted sum of linearized active lags. This may be better than max sum with hard constraints. We can solve:
minimize ∑_{k∈A} w_k (A_k g) subject to sum(g)=1, g≥0.
where A_k = row k of convolution matrix (f shift). This is a linear program (or linear program with equality) of minimizing p^T g, p=A^T w. The solution is g at a vertex: put all mass on coordinate with minimal p_j (if no other constraints), giving sparse extreme point. But with equality sum=1 and nonneg, min linear is one-hot at argmin p. That's too sparse. Add regularization? If we allow continuous, LP extreme is vertex. Not good. Previous maximizing sum with constraints has volume direction.

Could solve QP: minimize weighted active linearization + (γ/2)||g||^2 subject sum=1,g≥0. This is convex QP (with box/equality), has unique smooth solution. It gives a direction balancing reduction. The QP can be solved by projected gradient or `scipy.optimize.minimize`? We can derive: minimize ∑_{k} w_k (f*g)_k + (γ/2)∑ g_j^2 + λ(∑g-1). Equality sum. This is a quadratic program with diagonal plus linear term. We can solve via cyclic coordinate or use `scipy.optimize.minimize`? Actually with sum constraint, we can eliminate one variable or use water-filling: minimize ∑ p_j g_j + (γ/2)∑ g_j^2 + λ∑g_j, g≥0, sum=1. The solution is g_j = max((μ - p_j)/γ, 0), with μ chosen sum=1. This is O(n log n)! This is a Frank-Wolfe-like quadratic proximal direction. Much faster than LP and more flexible. Great.

Let's develop this! Given current f, choose weights w_k (nonnegative) focusing on active/high lags. Define linearized cost for direction g: p = A^T w (length n), where p_j = ∑_k w_k f_{k-j} (the derivative/linear term 2 f*g scaled). We want choose g≥0, sum=1 to minimize p^T g + (γ/2)||g||^2 (or perhaps ||g-f||^2? Norm regularization). The solution by water-filling:
- Minimize ∑ p_j g_j + (γ/2)∑ g_j^2 + λ(∑g -1). KKT: g_j = max((λ - p_j)/γ, 0). Choose λ so sum g=1.
This is easy. If p comparable, g smooths. If want direction towards lower linearized cost, λ positive. This generalizes previous LP? LP maximize sum g s.t. A g ≤ M is equivalent to find largest scaling of vector achieving constraints. QP direction maybe more stable.

We can then blend f ← (1-t)f + t g. Since objective quadratic, we can line search. This is a type of Frank-Wolfe with quadratic kernel (DCA?). It can be computed fast: need p = correlation? p_j = ∑_k w_k f_{k-j}. This is correlation of w with f (same as gradient without x? Actually gradient of w w.r.t f is 2 p). We already compute gradient with weights w and x=f. So p = grad_weights_x(w, f) (without 2). Great. So QP direction from weights can be computed with one FFT and water-filling O(n log n). This is much faster than LP and can be run many iterations. This is a significant different algorithm!

Let's formalize:
Objective Φ(f)=max_k c_k(f) (with sum fixed). At f, choose weights w_k (e.g., softmax of c) and linearization Φ(f+α(g-f)) ≈ Φ(f)+α∑_k w_k 2(f*g - f*f)? Actually if we consider moving to g convex combo with f, new sequence x=(1-α)f+αg. The active lag change linear: c_k(x) ≈ c_k(f)+2α∑_j (g_j-f_j) f_{k-j}. The cost direction d=g-f has p^T d =? p_j=∑w_k f_{k-j}; cost =∑ w_k 2 f*(g-f) = 2(p^T g - p^T f). Minimizing p^T g over g in simplex is what QP direction does. This is exactly Frank-Wolfe for a surrogate plus quadratic proximal regularizer. It should reduce active lags.

The QP water-filling solution g = argmin p^T g + (γ/2)||g||^2 s.t. sum=1,g≥0. If p is high where current f has high values? Need choose γ to control how sparse/g close to uniform. Smaller γ -> more extreme low-p coordinate; larger γ -> smoother. This may outperform LP. We can adapt γ.

This is promising and cheap. We can run many iterations with line search on true score. We can also use weights = softmax(c) or top active. The direction g is in simplex (sum1). It doesn't subtract f; we blend.

KKT water-filling:
Minimize (1/2)γ||g||^2 + p^T g + λ(∑g-1). For γ>0, g_j = max((λ - p_j)/γ,0). Need sum=1. We can solve by sorting p. Let values p sorted ascending. For a candidate number m of active (g>0), λ = (γ + sum_{active} p_j)/m? Derive: sum max(λ - p_j,0)/γ=1. Suppose active set A with p_j < λ. Then ∑_{j∈A}(λ-p_j)=γ => mλ - sum_p = γ => λ=(sum_p+γ)/m. Need condition p_max_active < λ ≤ p_min_inactive. Equivalent. We can implement by sorting p ascending and cumulative sums. For each m, average = (cumsum[m-1]+γ)/m; if average > p[m-1] and (m==n or average <= p[m]), then λ=average. Then g=(λ-p)/γ positive clipped, normalize. Actually if sum computed exactly should be 1. Complexity O(n log n) per QP direction. n=1024 okay. Can do 10k iterations.

What if p has large negative values? p = weighted f shifted; f nonnegative, weights nonnegative -> p≥0. Good. λ positive. If γ large relative p, g nearly uniform. If γ small, g concentrated on smallest p_j.

This QP direction is like Euclidean mirror descent. We can combine with momentum? Since g is already a whole new point in simplex, we can do line search blending α from f to g. No need gradient direction separately. This may be powerful.

We can choose weights w to focus on max: w = softmax(c/T) or w = c^{p-1}/sum. The p vector (sensitivity) might have structure. We can also include flat weights: w = softmax(η*c + (1-η)*flat)? Actually weights are nonnegative; flat weights c-mean can be negative not allowed for linearized cost. For reducing max, use nonnegative peak weights. The flat term can be approximated by adding small uniform weight over all lags: w = peak_weights + ε*ones. Since sum(c) fixed, uniform weight linear cost ∑ f*g_j = (sum f)(sum g)=1? For any g sum1, p_uniform_j = sum f =1 constant, so it doesn't affect direction (constant p). Thus uniform weights irrelevant. To encourage raising low lags, can't use negative linear weights in this QP? Maybe use weights based on target deficiency? But linear cost minimization only reduces weighted active lags; it won't proactively raise low lags except as side effect. The flat L2 gradient had negative weights to raise low lags. The QP direction may not equalize low lags. But maybe moving from f to low-p g naturally flattens, as LP did. We can add a secondary objective: minimize p^T g + δ ∑ (g_j - f_j)^2? Let's implement water-filling carefully.

```
def waterfill_direction(p, theta):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    pmin = p.min(); pmax = p.max()
    if not np.isfinite(pmin) or not np.isfinite(pmax) or pmax - pmin <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(p.size, 1.0/p.size)
    q = (p - pmin) / (pmax - pmin)  # [0,1]
    idx = np.argsort(q)
    qsorted = q[idx]
    cumsum = np.cumsum(qsorted)
    n = q.size
    # Find m
    # We need lambda = (theta + cumsum[m-1]) / m, condition qsorted[m-1] < lambda <= qsorted[m]
    # theta >0. If theta very large, m=n maybe.
    # We can vectorize:
    m = np.arange(1,n+1)
    lam = (theta + cumsum)/(m)
    cond = (lam > qsorted) & ((m == n) | (lam <= np.concatenate(([0.0], qsorted[:-1]))? Wait condition lam <= qsorted[m] for next smallest. For m active, next index m (0-based m) is qsorted[m] if m<n. Need lam <= qsorted[m]. For m=n, always. Use next_q = np.empty(n); next_q[:-1]=qsorted[1:]; next_q[-1]=np.inf. cond = (lam >= qsorted) & (lam <= next_q). But due float, use >= -1e-12.
    # There should be at least one. Choose first true? S(λ) increasing; the valid m unique. Use np.where.
    valid = np.where(cond)[0]
    if len(valid)==0: fallback m=n lam=(theta+cumsum[-1])/n
    else: m0=valid[0]
    lmbda = (theta + cumsum[m0])/(m0+1)
    gsorted = np.maximum((lmbda - qsorted)/theta, 0.0)
    g = np.empty_like(gsorted)
    g[idx] = gsorted
    s = g.sum()
    if s <= 0: return uniform
    return g/s
```

Let's implement `proximal_qo_optimize` (QP optimizer):

```
def proximal_qo_optimize(x0, deadline, eta0=0.3, eta_end=0.05, w_peak0=0.5, w_peak_end=1.0, w_flat0=1.0, w_flat_end=0.3, temp0=0.5, temp_end=0.1, alpha0=0.2, label=""):
    x=normalize
    best=x; best_score=score
    t_start=time.monotonic(); t_end=deadline; total=max(1e-6,t_end-t_start)
    alpha=alpha0
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time<deadline:
       frac=(time-t_start)/total
       eta = eta0 + (eta_end-eta0)*frac
       wp = w_peak0 + (w_peak_end-w_peak0)*frac
       wf = w_flat0 + (w_flat_end-w_flat0)*frac
       temp = temp0 + (temp_end-temp0)*frac
       c=conv_fft(x, fft_len?)
       # if current score better update

def get_fft_len(n):
    if n not in _FFT_CACHE:

def build_conv_matrix(f):
    n=len(f)

    diags = [np.full(n, f[d], dtype=float) for d in range(n)]

LP:
```
def solve_lp_direction(f, M, time_limit=10):
    if len(f)>1536: return None
    A=build_conv_matrix(f)
    c=-np.ones(len(f))
    b_ub=np.full(2*len(f)-1, M)
    bounds=[(0,None)]*len(f)
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': time_limit, 'output_flag': False})
       if res.success and res.x is not None: return np.clip(res.x,0,None)
    except Exception: pass
    return None
```
`linprog` objective minimize c^T x; c=-ones maximize sum. Good.

      else: inits.append(('parent_interp', interp_to_length(parent_arr,n)))
   inits.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)/n))
   dec = sqrt_flat_sequence(n); inits.append(('dec', dec))
   inc = dec[::-1]; inits.append(('inc', inc))
   avg = normalize_sum((dec+inc)/2); inits.append(('avg_mix', avg))
   for beta in [0.35,0.5,0.65]: inits append power law
   for gamma in [1,2]: exp decay
   # random smooth low frequency

Interpolation:
```
def interp_to_length(x, n):
    m=len(x)
    if m==n: return x.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,m)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    y=np.interp(x_new, x_old, x)
    return y
```

Let's implement `line_search_segment(f, g, A, B, C, n, best_score, candidate_alphas)`:
```
current_score = 2*n*max(A) # if sum1
best_a=0; best_s=best_score
for a in candidate_alphas:
   conv = (1-a)**2*A + 2*a*(1-a)*C + a*a*B
   s = 2*n*max(conv) # sum1
   if s < best_s: update
if best_a>0: cand = (1-a)*f + a*g
return cand,best_a,best_s
```

Pseudo code final:
```
SEGMENT_ALPHAS = [...]  # top-level? Could not define as constant? yes.
FFT_CACHE = {}

def qo_optimize(x0, deadline, eta0=0.5, eta1=0.15, wp0=0.6, wp1=1.0, wf0=1.0, wf1=0.6, temp0=0.5, temp1=0.15, label="", rng=None):
    global? no
    x = normalize_sum(x0.copy(),1.0)
    n=len(x); L=2*n-1; N=get_fft_len(n)
    best_x=x.copy(); best_score=score_fast(x)
    t0=time.monotonic(); total=max(1e-6, deadline - t0)
    no_improve=0; it=0
    alphas=SEGMENT_ALPHAS
    last_a=-1
    stuck_phase=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it+=1
        # interpolated params (unless stuck)
        frac=(time.monotonic()-t0)/total
        eta=eta0+(eta1-eta0)*frac; ...
        if no_improve>80: apply stuck overrides maybe
        Fx=np.fft.rfft(x,N)
        A=np.fft.irfft(Fx*Fx,N)[:L].real
        maxA=float(A.max()); s_cur=2*n*maxA # sum 1
        if s_cur < best_score - 1e-15:
             best_score=s_cur; best_x=x.copy()
        # if maxA? target = 1/(2n-1)
        target=1.0/L
        maxc=maxA
        if maxc <= target: break # near lower bound? score 1
        temp=max(temp_val*(maxc-target), 1e-12*maxc)
        z=(A - maxc)/temp
        z=np.clip(z, -100, 0)
        peak=np.exp(z); peak_sum=peak.sum(); peak/=peak_sum
        flat=A-target
        flat_l1=float(np.abs(flat).sum())
        if flat_l1 > 1e-30:
            flat_n=flat/flat_l1
        else: flat_n=np.zeros(L)
        w=wp_val*peak + wf_val*flat_n
        # If wf_val==0 and wp_val==0? avoid
        Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
        p=np.fft.irfft(np.conjugate(Fw)*Fx,N)[:n].real
        pmax=p.max(); pmin=p.min(); prange=pmax-pmin
        if prange < 1e-12: break
        g=waterfill_direction(p, eta_val)
        # maybe if g too close to x: if np.linalg.norm(g-x)<1e-5 break?
        Fg=np.fft.rfft(g,N)
        B=np.fft.irfft(Fg*Fg,N)[:L].real
        C=np.fft.irfft(Fx*Fg,N)[:L].real
        # line search
        best_a=0.0; best_s=s_cur; best_conv=None
        for idx,a in enumerate(alphas):
             if a<=0: continue
             if a==1: Cterm=0? formula works
             conv = (1-a)*(1-a)*A + 2*a*(1-a)*C + a*a*B
             s=2*n*float(conv.max())
             if s < best_s - 1e-15:
                 best_s=s; best_a=a
        # local refine around best_a
        if best_a > 0:
             # find neighbors
             lo=0.0; hi=1.0
             for i,a in enumerate(alphas):
                if abs(a-best_a)<1e-12:
                   lo=alphas[i-1] if i>0 else 0.0
                   hi=alphas[i+1] if i<len(alphas)-1 else 1.0
                   break
             # also if best_s at very small, include 0? local range
             if hi-lo > 0:
                 for a in np.linspace(lo,hi,11)[1:-1]:
                     conv=...
                     s=...
                     if s<best_s: update
        if best_a > 0 and best_s < s_cur - 1e-15:
             x = (1-best_a)*x + best_a*g
             x = normalize_sum(x,1.0) # just in case
             best_x = x.copy(); best_score = best_s
             no_improve=0
             # optionally if best_a==1 and next? no
        else:
             no_improve +=1
             if no_improve == 50:
                 # temporary sharp peak
             elif no_improve == 100:
                 # smooth flat
             elif no_improve > 200:
                 # restart with perturbation of best if rng
                 base=best_x.copy()
                 if rng is not None:
                    noise=rng.normal(0,0.02,n)
                 else: noise=0.02*np.sin(np.linspace(0,50,n))
                 x=normalize_sum(base+noise,1.0)
                 no_improve=0
                 stuck_phase=0
                 continue
             # if no_improve > 2000 break?
             if no_improve > 2000: break
    return best_x,best_score
```

`sqrt_flat_sequence` O(n^2). For n=2048, 2M dot operations; using numpy dot per k. Let's implement efficient:
```
def sqrt_flat_sequence(n):
    alpha = 1.0/(2*n-1)
    x=np.zeros(n)
    x[0]=np.sqrt(alpha)
    x0=x[0]
    for k in range(1,n):
        if k == 1:
            s=0.0
        else:
            s = float(np.dot(x[1:k], x[k-1:0:-1]))
        val = (alpha - s)/(2*x0)
        if val < 0: val=0.0
        x[k]=val
    return normalize_sum(x,1.0)
```
But if we clip negative, subsequent s uses clipped, and c0 still alpha? x0 fixed. Sum then normalize. Good.

This sequence based on flat early target with alpha=1/(2n-1). For n=2048, α~0.000244, x0=0.0156. Dot operations sum k ~2M; numpy dot each k with length up to 2047, total 2M C operations but 2048 Python calls; maybe 0.05s. For multiple n maybe okay. Could compute with recurrence using cumulative cross? Not needed.

But is `s` correct after clipping? For target α with x0 fixed, x_k=(α - ∑_{j=1}^{k-1}x_j x_{k-j})/(2x0). For k=2, dot x[1:2] with x[1:0:-1] length1 x1*x1 correct. Good.

`power_law_sequence`:
```
def power_law_sequence(n, beta, shift=0.5, reverse=False):
    idx=np.arange(n, dtype=float)+shift
    x=np.power(idx, -beta)
    if reverse: x=x[::-1]
    x=np.clip(x,1e-12,None)
    return normalize_sum(x)
```
For beta=0.0, all1. For beta negative? no.

```
def generate_initializations(n, parent, rng):
    inits=[]
    if parent is not None:
       p=normalize_sum(parent.astype(float).copy(),1.0)
       if len(p)==n: inits.append(('parent',p))
       else: inits.append(('parent_interp', normalize_sum(interp_to_length(p,n),1.0)))
    inits.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)/n))
    dec=sqrt_flat_sequence(n)
    inits.append(('dec',dec))
    inc=dec[::-1].copy()
    inits.append(('inc',normalize_sum(inc)))
    avg=normalize_sum(0.5*(dec+inc))
    inits.append(('avg',avg))
    # mix dec with avg for t
    inits.append(('mix03', normalize_sum(0.7*dec+0.3*inc)))
    inits.append(('mix50', avg)) # duplicate maybe remove
    for beta in [0.35,0.5,0.65]:
       inits.append((f'pow_{beta}', power_law_sequence(n,beta)))
       inits.append((f'powrev_{beta}', power_law_sequence(n,beta,reverse=True)))
    for gamma in [1.0,2.0]:
       inits.append((f'exp_{gamma}', exp_decay_sequence(n,gamma)))
    # random smooth: low freq
    # Use rng
    if rng is not None:
       # generate random Fourier coefficients low frequency
       k=np.arange(1,n//4+1)
       phases=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi,k.size)
       amps=rng.uniform(0.1,0.5,k.size)/(k**0.5)
       sig=0.5 + np.sum(0.1*? )? 
```

```
def lp_polish(x0, deadline, max_calls=30, label=""):
    x=normalize_sum(x0.copy(),1.0)
    n=len(x); N=get_fft_len(n); L=2*n-1
    best_x=x; best_score=score_fast(x)
    no=0; calls=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        calls+=1
        Fx=rfft(x,N); A=irfft(Fx*Fx,N)[:L].real
        maxA=A.max(); s_cur=2*n*maxA
        if s_cur<best_score: update
        # M can be maxA * (1.0)? LP constraints f*g <= M. If M maxA, g=f feasible.
        g=solve_lp_direction(x, maxA, time_limit=5)
        if g is None:
           no+=1
           if no>3: break
           continue
        g=normalize_sum(g,1.0)
        if np.linalg.norm(g-x) < 1e-6: break
        Fg=rfft(g,N); B=...; C=...
        # line search same as qo
        best_a=0; best_s=s_cur
        for a in SEGMENT_ALPHAS: ...
        local refine
        if best_a>0 and best_s < s_cur:
             x=...; best update; no=0
        else:
             no+=1
             if no>5: break
        if calls>=max_calls: break
    return best_x,best_score
```
LP constraints use `M = maxA` (since sum1). If x sum not exactly1, M = maxA/(sum^2)? Actually f*g linear uses f values, g sum1. The current f*f max = maxA. Feasible g=f? f*g = f*f = A <= M yes. Sum f=1. Good.

If x not sum1 exactly, normalize before, so sum1.

`qo_optimize` details in code:
```
def qo_optimize(x0, deadline, eta0=0.5, eta1=0.15, wp0=0.6, wp1=1.0, wf0=1.0, wf1=0.5, temp0=0.5, temp1=0.15, label="", rng=None):
    x = normalize_sum(np.asarray(x0, dtype=float).copy(), 1.0)
    n = len(x)
    N = get_fft_len(n)
    L = 2*n - 1
    best_x = x.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(x)
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(1e-9, float(deadline) - t_start)
    alphas = _SEGMENT_ALPHAS
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        it += 1
        frac = (time.monotonic() - t_start) / total
        frac = min(1.0, max(0.0, frac))
        eta_val = eta0 + (eta1 - eta0) * frac
        wp_val = wp0 + (wp1 - wp0) * frac
        wf_val = wf0 + (wf1 - wf0) * frac
        temp_val = temp0 + (temp1 - temp0) * frac
        if no_improve > 30 and no_improve < 90:
            eta_val = max(0.03, min(eta_val * 0.25, 0.1))
            wp_val = 1.0
            wf_val = 0.0
            temp_val = min(0.1, temp_val)
        elif no_improve >= 90 and no_improve < 160:
            eta_val = max(0.5, eta_val * 2.0)
            wp_val = 0.2
            wf_val = 1.0
            temp_val = max(0.2, temp_val)
        elif no_improve >= 160:
            if rng is not None:
                r = float(rng.random())
            else:
                r = 0.5
            eta_val = 0.05 + 0.7*r
            wp_val = 1.0
            wf_val = 0.5*r
            temp_val = 0.1 + 0.3*r
            if no_improve % 50 == 0:
                # perturbation of best
                base = best_x.copy()
                if rng is not None:
                    noise = 0.01 * rng.standard_normal(n)
                else:
                    noise = 0.01 * np.sin(np.arange(n) * 0.21 * it)
                x = normalize_sum(base + noise, 1.0)
                no_improve = 20  # keep in random region? maybe reset to 0? If reset, schedule normal. We want try different. Set 10.
                no_improve = 30
        Fx = np.fft.rfft(x, N)
        A = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Fx, N)[:L].real
        maxA = float(A.max())
        s_cur = 2.0 * n * maxA
        if s_cur < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = s_cur
            best_x = x.copy()
        target = 1.0 / L
        if maxA <= target * (1.0 + 1e-9):
            break
        temp_span = temp_val * (maxA - target)
        if temp_span < 1e-14:
            temp_span = 1e-12 * maxA
        z = (A - maxA) / temp_span
        z = np.clip(z, -100.0, 0.0)
        peak = np.exp(z)
        psum = float(peak.sum())
        if psum > 0: peak /= psum
        else: peak[:] = 1.0 / L
        flat = A - target
        flat_l1 = float(np.abs(flat).sum())
        if flat_l1 > 1e-30:
            flat_n = flat / flat_l1
        else:
            flat_n = np.zeros(L)
        if wf_val != 0.0:
            w = wp_val * peak + wf_val * flat_n
        else:
            w = peak.copy()
        # if wp_val 0 and wf 0? not
        Fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
        p = np.fft.irfft(np.conjugate(Fw) * Fx, N)[:n].real
        pmin = float(p.min()); pmax = float(p.max()); prange = pmax - pmin
        if prange < 1e-13:
            no_improve += 5
            if no_improve > 200: break
            continue
        g = waterfill_direction(p, eta_val)
        # if g close, maybe no improve
        Fg = np.fft.rfft(g, N)
        B = np.fft.irfft(Fg * Fg, N)[:L].real
        C = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Fg, N)[:L].real
        best_a = 0.0
        best_s = s_cur
        # coarse search
        for a in alphas:
            if a <= 0.0: continue
            om = 1.0 - a
            conv = om*om*A + 2.0*a*om*C + a*a*B
            s = 2.0 * n * float(conv.max())
            if s < best_s - 1e-14:
                best_s = s; best_a = a
        # local refine
        if best_a > 0.0:
            lo = 0.0; hi = 1.0
            found = False
            for i,a in enumerate(alphas):
                if abs(a - best_a) < 1e-12:
                    lo = alphas[i-1] if i > 0 else 0.0
                    hi = alphas[i+1] if i+1 < len(alphas) else 1.0
                    found = True
                    break
            if found and hi > lo:
                for a in np.linspace(lo, hi, 11)[1:-1]:
                    if a <= 0.0 or a >= 1.0: continue
                    om=1.0-a
                    conv=...
                    s=...
                    if s<best_s: update
        if best_a <= 0.0 and maxA > 0:
            # derivative active maybe
            active = A >= maxA - 1e-12
            cmax_active = float(C[active].max()) if np.any(active) else 0.0
            if cmax_active < maxA - 1e-12:
                for a in (0.0002, 0.0005, 0.001, 0.002):
                   ...
        if best_a > 0.0 and best_s < s_cur - 1e-14:
            x = (1.0 - best_a) * x + best_a * g
            x = normalize_sum(x, 1.0)
            best_x = x.copy(); best_score = best_s
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 10000:
                break
    return best_x, best_score
```
This code repeated for derivative; okay.

`waterfill_direction` with p length n, eta. Let's code robust:
```
def waterfill_direction(p, eta):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    pmin = float(p.min()); pmax = float(p.max())
    if not np.isfinite(pmin) or not np.isfinite(pmax) or (pmax - pmin) <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(n, 1.0/n)
    q = (p - pmin) / (pmax - pmin)
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=float)
    order = np.argsort(q)
    qsort = q[order]
    cum = np.cumsum(qsort)
    target = float(n) * float(eta)
    if target <= 0: target = 1e-6
    m_vals = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=float)
    lam = (target + cum) / m_vals
    next_q = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    if n > 1:
        next_q[:-1] = qsort[1:]
    next_q[-1] = np.inf
    tol = 1e-12
    valid = (lam >= qsort - tol) & (lam <= next_q + tol)
    idxs = np.where(valid)[0]
    if idxs.size == 0:
        # fallback choose highest m? or binary? use argmax of condition? fallback n
        m0 = n - 1
    else:
        m0 = int(idxs[0])
    lmbda = (target + cum[m0]) / (m0 + 1)
    gsort = np.maximum((lmbda - qsort) / target, 0.0)
    s = float(gsort.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n, 1.0/n)
    gsort /= s
    g = np.empty_like(gsort)
    g[order] = gsort
    g /= g.sum() # normalize
    return g
```
Check factor: g = (λ - q)/target, sum should 1 by λ definition if active set correct. But due clipping/inactivity, divide. Good.

If eta huge, lam may be inf? target n*eta maybe up to n*10=10240; cum ~n; lam ~11; finite. If eta set 100, lam ~100; fine. We'll not set huge.

Now, `score_fast` uses `conv_fft` with `get_fft_len`. For n=1? not relevant. Implementation:
```
def score_fast(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    n=x.size
    sumx=float(x.sum())
    if n==0 or sumx <=1e-15: return float('inf')
    N=get_fft_len(n)
    F=np.fft.rfft(x,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1].real
    return float(2*n*float(c.max())/(sumx*sumx))
```
For normalized sum1. If x has small negatives? normalize clips. Good.

`normalize_sum`:
```
def normalize_sum(x, target=1.0):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float).copy()
    x=np.clip(x,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(x.shape, target/max(1,x.size), dtype=float)
    x = x * (target/s)
    return np.clip(x,0.0,1000.0)
```
If target=1, entries <1. Good.

rows=[]; cols=[]; vals=[]
for d in range(n):

```
This uses Python loop n with vector arrays; memory similar but safe. For n=1024, 1024 loops, okay. For n=2048, 2048 loops, okay. Use `scipy.sparse.coo_matrix`. But creating list of arrays each d maybe. This avoids offset ambiguity. Let's do explicit COO with preallocated? Simpler:
```
def build_conv_matrix(f):
    n=len(f)
    rows_all=[]; cols_all=[]; vals_all=[]
    j_base=np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32)
    for d in range(n):
        rows_all.append(j_base + d)
        cols_all.append(j_base)
        vals_all.append(np.full(n, f[d], dtype=float))
    rows=np.concatenate(rows_all); cols=np.concatenate(cols_all); vals=np.concatenate(vals_all)
    return coo_matrix((vals,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n)).tocsr()
```
This is clear but memory temporary: rows n*n ints (1M*4=4MB), cols 4MB, vals 8MB => okay. For 2048: 4M each ->64MB, maybe okay. Use only n<=1536 to be safe. We'll use explicit COO. Need import `coo_matrix`.

Global `_FFT_CACHE = {}`, `_SEGMENT_ALPHAS = (...)`.

`get_fft_len`:
```
def get_fft_len(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n in _FFT_CACHE:
        return _FFT_CACHE[n]
    L = 3*n - 2
    if L < 1: L=1
    f = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    _FFT_CACHE[n] = f
    return f
```
If L is power of two? E.g., L=1 -> (0).bit_length()=0 ->1<<0=1. L=2048? If 3n-2 maybe can be power? Usually not; formula works: if L=2048, L-1=2047 bit_length11 ->2048. Good.

`score_fast`:
```
def score_fast(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    n=x.size
    if n==0: return inf
    s=float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s): return inf
    N=get_fft_len(n)
    F=np.fft.rfft(x,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1].real
    if c.size==0: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*float(c.max())/(s*s))
```

`interp_to_length`:
```
def interp_to_length(x,n):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    m=x.size
    if m==0: return np.ones(n)
    if m==n: return x.copy()
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    return np.interp(xn,xo,x)
```

`power_law_sequence`: If beta==0, np.power idx 0 =1. If negative? no. Use `np.clip(x,1e-300,None)`.

`exp_decay_sequence`.

`generate_initializations`: Use `labels` maybe. Need avoid duplicate `avg` and `mix50` same. Code:
```
def generate_initializations(n, parent, rng):
    inits=[]
    if parent is not None:
       p=normalize_sum(parent,1.0)
       if p.size==n: seq=p.copy()
       else: seq=normalize_sum(interp_to_length(p,n),1.0)
       inits.append(('parent',seq))
    inits.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)/float(n)))
    dec=sqrt_flat_sequence(n)
    inits.append(('dec',dec))
    inc=dec[::-1].copy(); inits.append(('inc', normalize_sum(inc,1.0)))
    avg=normalize_sum(0.5*dec + 0.5*inc,1.0); inits.append(('avg',avg))
    mix3=normalize_sum(0.7*dec + 0.3*inc,1.0); inits.append(('mix3',mix3))
    mix5=avg # skip duplicate
    for beta in (0.35,0.5,0.65):
       inits.append((('pow'+str(beta)), power_law_sequence(n,beta)))
       inits.append((('powrev'+str(beta)), power_law_sequence(n,beta,reverse=True)))
    for gamma in (1.0,2.0):
       inits.append(('exp'+str(gamma), exp_decay_sequence(n,gamma)))
       inits.append(('exprev'+str(gamma), exp_decay_sequence(n,gamma,reverse=True)))
    if rng is not None:
       r=np.asarray(rng.standard_normal(n), dtype=float)
       klen=min(50,n)
       if klen>1:
          kernel=np.ones(klen)/float(klen)
          r=np.convolve(r, kernel, mode='same')
       r=1.0 + 0.5*r
       inits.append(('rand', normalize_sum(r,1.0)))
    # normalize all
    out=[]
    for label, seq in inits:
       if seq.size==n:
          out.append((label, normalize_sum(seq,1.0)))
    return out
```
String beta with dot in label okay.

```
def line_search_segment(A,B,C,n,baseline):
    best_a=0.0; best_s=baseline
    for a in _SEGMENT_ALPHAS:
       if a<=0: continue
       om=1-a
       s=2*n*float(np.max(om*om*A + 2*a*om*C + a*a*B))
       if s < best_s - 1e-14: best_s=s; best_a=a
    if best_a>0:
       find neighbors, refine 11
    # derivative small
    if best_a==0:
       maxA=float(A.max())
       active = A >= maxA - 1e-12
       if active.any():
          cmax=float(C[active].max())
          if cmax < maxA - 1e-12:
             for a in (0.0001,0.0003,0.0006,0.001,0.002): ...
    return best_a,best_s
```
This code uses `active.any()`. Good. It avoids duplication. Function call per iteration (10k) okay? One Python call per iter negligible compared to numpy. Inside uses temp arrays. Good.

Now, one more thought: The QO direction uses p=corr(w,x). But waterfill minimizes p^T g + eta||g||^2. If p has negative values, q normalized min=0; waterfill assigns g to low p. This is equivalent to min p^T g with quadratic norm scaled by eta. Good. If p all very large positive except a few negative, q low for negative. eta controls spread.

Now, final code.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2.

The main method is a fast Frank-Wolfe / proximal-gradient style iteration:
  - work on the simplex sum(a)=1, a>=0;
  - form signed weights of the current autocorrelation:
      softmax peak weights + signed flatness weights (c - mean);
  - compute the linearized sensitivity p = corr(weights, current);
  - choose a new simplex point by water-filling min p^T g + eta||g||^2;
  - line-search along the whole segment current -> g using FFT convolutions.

Secondary acceleration comes from LP directions and structured initializations
(recursive square-root-of-flat early autocorrelation, power laws, exponentials).
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.sparse import coo_matrix
from scipy.optimize import linprog

_FFT_CACHE = {}
_SEGMENT_ALPHAS = (
    0.001, 0.003, 0.01, 0.03, 0.06, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20,
    0.30, 0.45, 0.60, 0.75, 0.90, 1.00
)


def get_fft_len(n):
    """Next power of two sufficient for conv(a,a) and corr(w,a)."""
    n = int(n)
    if n in _FFT_CACHE:
        return _FFT_CACHE[n]
    L = 3 * n - 2
    if L < 1:
        L = 1
    f = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    _FFT_CACHE[n] = f
    return f


def normalize_sum(x, target=1.0):
    """Clip to non-negative and scale to target sum."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float).copy()
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(x.shape, target / float(max(1, x.size)), dtype=float)
    x = x * (target / s)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return x


def score_fast(x):
    """Internal objective, accounting for arbitrary scale."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return float("inf")
    N = get_fft_len(n)
    F = np.fft.rfft(x, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)[:2 * n - 1].real
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s))


def interp_to_length(x, n):
    """Linearly resample a one-dimensional sequence to length n."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    m = x.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=float)
    if m == n:
        return x.copy()
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return np.interp(xn, xo, x)


def sqrt_flat_sequence(n):
    """
    Initialize from formal square root of a flat early autocorrelation:
    x_i are the first n coefficients of sqrt(alpha/(1-z)) plus normalization.
    """
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    alpha = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)
    x = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    x[0] = float(np.sqrt(alpha))
    x0 = x[0]
    if x0 <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=float)

    for k in range(1, n):
        if k == 1:
            s = 0.0
        else:
            s = float(np.dot(x[1:k], x[k - 1:0:-1]))
        val = (alpha - s) / (2.0 * x0)
        if not np.isfinite(val) or val < 0.0:
            val = 0.0
        x[k] = val
    return normalize_sum(x, 1.0)


def power_law_sequence(n, beta, shift=0.5, reverse=False):
    """Simple decreasing/increasing power-law initialization."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=float) + float(shift)
    x = np.power(idx, -float(beta), where=idx > 0)
    x = np.clip(x, 1e-300, None)
    if reverse:
        x = x[::-1]
    return normalize_sum(x, 1.0)


def exp_decay_sequence(n, gamma, reverse=False):
    """Exponential taper initialization."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x = np.exp(-float(gamma) * t)
    if reverse:
        x = x[::-1]
    return normalize_sum(x, 1.0)


def generate_initializations(n, parent, rng):
    """Make a diverse set of initial simplex vectors of length n."""
    n = int(n)
    inits = []

    if parent is not None:
        p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=float)
        p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if p.size > 0 and float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
            p = normalize_sum(p, 1.0)
            if p.size == n:
                inits.append(("parent", p.copy()))
            else:
                inits.append(("parent_interp", normalize_sum(interp_to_length(p, n), 1.0)))

    inits.append(("uniform", normalize_sum(np.ones(n, dtype=float), 1.0)))

    dec = sqrt_flat_sequence(n)
    if dec.size == n:
        inits.append(("dec_sqrt", dec))
        incs = dec[::-1].copy()
        inits.append(("inc_sqrt", normalize_sum(incs, 1.0)))
        avg = normalize_sum(0.5 * dec + 0.5 * incs, 1.0)
        inits.append(("avg_sqrt", avg))
        mix = normalize_sum(0.70 * dec + 0.30 * incs, 1.0)
        inits.append(("mix_sqrt", mix))

    for beta in (0.35, 0.50, 0.65):
        inits.append(("pow_%s" % beta, power_law_sequence(n, beta)))
        inits.append(("powrev_%s" % beta, power_law_sequence(n, beta, reverse=True)))

    for gamma in (1.0, 2.0):
        inits.append(("exp_%s" % gamma, exp_decay_sequence(n, gamma)))
        inits.append(("exprev_%s" % gamma, exp_decay_sequence(n, gamma, reverse=True)))

    if rng is not None:
        r = rng.standard_normal(n).astype(float)
        klen = min(50, n)
        if klen > 1:
            kernel = np.ones(klen, dtype=float) / float(klen)
            r = np.convolve(r, kernel, mode="same")
        r = 1.0 + 0.5 * r
        inits.append(("smooth_random", normalize_sum(r, 1.0)))

    out = []
    for label, seq in inits:
        seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=float)
        if seq.size == n:
            out.append((label, normalize_sum(seq, 1.0)))
    return out


def waterfill_direction(p, eta):
    """
    Solve: g = argmin_{g>=0, sum(g)=1} <p,g> + (n*eta)/2 ||g||^2.
    The solution is water-filling over the scaled coordinates p.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)

    pmin = float(np.min(p))
    pmax = float(np.max(p))
    if not np.isfinite(pmin) or not np.isfinite(pmax) or (pmax - pmin) <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=float)

    q = (p - pmin) / (pmax - pmin)
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=float)
    order = np.argsort(q)
    qs = q[order]
    cum = np.cumsum(qs)
    target = float(n) * float(eta)
    if target <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(target):
        target = 1e-6

    mvals = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=float)
    lam = (target + cum) / mvals
    nxt = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    if n > 1:
        nxt[:-1] = qs[1:]
    nxt[-1] = np.inf

    tol = 1e-12
    valid = (lam >= qs - tol) & (lam <= nxt + tol)
    idxs = np.where(valid)[0]
    if idxs.size == 0:
        m0 = n - 1
    else:
        m0 = int(idxs[0])

    lmbda = (target + cum[m0]) / float(m0 + 1)
    gs = np.maximum((lmbda - qs) / target, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(gs))
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=float)
    gs = gs / s

    g = np.empty_like(gs)
    g[order] = gs
    s2 = float(np.sum(g))
    if s2 > 1e-15 and np.isfinite(s2):
        g = g / s2
    else:
        g = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=float)
    return g


def line_search_segment(A, B, C, n, baseline):
    """
    Search alpha in [0,1] for min max(conv((1-alpha)x+alpha g)) under sum=1,
    given A=conv(x,x), B=conv(g,g), C=conv(x,g).
    Returns (alpha, score).
    """
    best_a = 0.0
    best_s = baseline

    # Coarse segment search.
    for a in _SEGMENT_ALPHAS:
        if a <= 0.0:
            continue
        om = 1.0 - a
        fault = om * om * A + (2.0 * a * om) * C + (a * a) * B
        s = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(fault))
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s - 1e-14:
            best_s = s
            best_a = float(a)

    # Local refinement around the best coarse alpha.
    if best_a > 0.0:
        lo = 0.0
        hi = 1.0
        found = False
        for i, a in enumerate(_SEGMENT_ALPHAS):
            if abs(float(a) - best_a) < 1e-12:
                lo = float(_SEGMENT_ALPHAS[i - 1]) if i > 0 else 0.0
                hi = float(_SEGMENT_ALPHAS[i + 1]) if i + 1 < len(_SEGMENT_ALPHAS) else 1.0
                found = True
                break
        if found and hi > lo:
            for a in np.linspace(lo, hi, 11)[1:-1]:
                a = float(a)
                if a <= 0.0 or a >= 1.0:
                    continue
                om = 1.0 - a
                fault = om * om * A + (2.0 * a * om) * C + (a * a) * B
                s = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(fault))
                if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s - 1e-14:
                    best_s = s
                    best_a = a

    # Very small alpha fallback based on directional derivative at the active max.
    if best_a <= 0.0:
        maxA = float(np.max(A))
        if maxA > 0.0:
            active = A >= maxA - 1e-12
            if np.any(active):
                cmax_active = float(np.max(C[active]))
                if cmax_active < maxA - 1e-12:
                    for a in (0.0001, 0.0003, 0.0006, 0.001, 0.002, 0.003, 0.005):
                        om = 1.0 - a
                        fault = om * om * A + (2.0 * a * om) * C + (a * a) * B
                        s = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(fault))
                        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s - 1e-14:
                            best_s = s
                            best_a = float(a)
                        if best_a > 0.0:
                            break

    return best_a, best_s


def qo_optimize(x0, deadline,
                eta0=0.35, eta1=0.08,
                wp0=0.80, wp1=1.00,
                wf0=0.60, wf1=0.30,
                temp0=0.30, temp1=0.08,
                label="", rng=None):
    """
    Frank-Wolfe / proximal water-filling optimizer.
    All points are kept on the simplex; line search is along x -> g.
    """
    x = normalize_sum(np.asarray(x0, dtype=float).copy(), 1.0)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return x, float("inf")

    N = get_fft_len(n)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    best_x = x.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(x)

    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(1e-9, float(deadline) - t_start)
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        it += 1
        frac = (time.monotonic() - t_start) / total
        if frac < 0.0:
            frac = 0.0
        if frac > 1.0:
            frac = 1.0

        eta_val = eta0 + (eta1 - eta0) * frac
        wp_val = wp0 + (wp1 - wp0) * frac
        wf_val = wf0 + (wf1 - wf0) * frac
        temp_val = temp0 + (temp1 - temp0) * frac

        # If stuck, temporarily switch to sharper or smoother directions.
        if 30 < no_improve < 90:
            eta_val = max(0.03, min(eta_val * 0.25, 0.10))
            wp_val = 1.0
            wf_val = 0.0
            temp_val = min(0.10, temp_val)
        elif 90 <= no_improve < 160:
            eta_val = max(0.50, eta_val * 2.0)
            wp_val = 0.25
            wf_val = 1.0
            temp_val = max(0.20, temp_val)
        elif no_improve >= 160:
            if rng is not None:
                r = float(rng.random())
            else:
                r = 0.5
            eta_val = 0.05 + 0.75 * r
            wp_val = 1.0
            wf_val = 0.55 * r
            temp_val = 0.08 + 0.22 * r
            if no_improve % 50 == 0:
                base = best_x.copy()
                if rng is not None:
                    noise = 0.012 * rng.standard_normal(n)
                else:
                    noise = 0.012 * np.sin(np.arange(n, dtype=float) * (0.19 * it + 0.7))
                x = normalize_sum(base + noise, 1.0)
                no_improve = 35

        Fx = np.fft.rfft(x, N)
        A = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Fx, N)[:L].real
        maxA = float(np.max(A))
        s_cur = 2.0 * float(n) * maxA

        if np.isfinite(s_cur) and s_cur < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = s_cur
            best_x = x.copy()

        target = 1.0 / float(L)
        if not np.isfinite(maxA) or maxA <= target * (1.0 + 1e-9):
            break

        span = temp_val * (maxA - target)
        if span < 1e-14:
            span = 1e-12 * maxA

        z = (A - maxA) / span
        z = np.clip(z, -100.0, 0.0)
        peak = np.exp(z)
        psum = float(np.sum(peak))
        if psum > 1e-300 and np.isfinite(psum):
            peak = peak / psum
        else:
            peak = np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=float)

        flat = A - target
        flat_l1 = float(np.sum(np.abs(flat)))
        flat_scale = max(flat_l1, 0.05)
        if flat_scale > 1e-300 and np.isfinite(flat_scale):
            flat_n = flat / flat_scale
        else:
            flat_n = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)

        if abs(wf_val) > 1e-12:
            w = wp_val * peak + wf_val * flat_n
        else:
            w = peak.copy()

        # Linearized sensitivity p_j = sum_k w_k x_{k-j}.
        Fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
        p = np.fft.irfft(np.conjugate(Fw) * Fx, N)[:n].real

        pmin = float(np.min(p))
        pmax = float(np.max(p))
        prange = pmax - pmin
        if not np.isfinite(prange) or prange < 1e-12:
            no_improve += 5
            if no_improve > 3000:
                break
            x = best_x.copy()
            continue

        g = waterfill_direction(p, eta_val)
        if g.size != n:
            break

        Fg = np.fft.rfft(g, N)
        B = np.fft.irfft(Fg * Fg, N)[:L].real
        C = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Fg, N)[:L].real

        a_star, s_star = line_search_segment(A, B, C, n, best_score)

        if a_star > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s_star) and s_star < best_score - 1e-14:
            x = (1.0 - a_star) * x + a_star * g
            x = normalize_sum(x, 1.0)
            best_score = s_star
            best_x = x.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            x = best_x.copy()
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 4000:
                break

    return best_x, best_score


def qo_optimize_main(x0, deadline, label="", rng=None):
    """Balanced peak + flatness configuration."""
    return qo_optimize(
        x0, deadline,
        eta0=0.35, eta1=0.08,
        wp0=0.80, wp1=1.00,
        wf0=0.60, wf1=0.30,
        temp0=0.30, temp1=0.08,
        label=label, rng=rng
    )


def qo_optimize_peak(x0, deadline, label="", rng=None):
    """Peak-only sharper configuration."""
    return qo_optimize(
        x0, deadline,
        eta0=0.20, eta1=0.03,
        wp0=1.00, wp1=1.00,
        wf0=0.00, wf1=0.00,
        temp0=0.25, temp1=0.05,
        label=label, rng=rng
    )


def qo_optimize_flat(x0, deadline, label="", rng=None):
    """Flatness-heavy configuration for reshaping."""
    return qo_optimize(
        x0, deadline,
        eta0=0.60, eta1=0.22,
        wp0=0.25, wp1=0.35,
        wf0=1.00, wf1=1.00,
        temp0=0.50, temp1=0.25,
        label=label, rng=rng
    )


def qo_optimize_fine(x0, deadline, label="", rng=None):
    """Very focused final polish."""
    return qo_optimize(
        x0, deadline,
        eta0=0.14, eta1=0.025,
        wp0=1.00, wp1=1.00,
        wf0=0.25, wf1=0.08,
        temp0=0.14, temp1=0.045,
        label=label, rng=rng
    )


def build_conv_matrix(f):
    """
    Sparse matrix A such that (A @ g) = convolve(f, g).
    Explicit COO construction avoids diagonal-offset ambiguity.
    """
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=float)
    n = f.size
    if n == 0:
        return coo_matrix((0, n), dtype=float)

    cols_all = []
    rows_all = []
    vals_all = []
    jbase = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)

    for d in range(n):
        k = jbase + d
        cols_all.append(jbase)
        rows_all.append(k)
        vals_all.append(np.full(n, float(f[d]), dtype=float))

    rows = np.concatenate(rows_all)
    cols = np.concatenate(cols_all)
    vals = np.concatenate(vals_all)
    A = coo_matrix((vals, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n), dtype=float)
    return A.tocsr()


def solve_lp_direction(f, M, time_limit=5.0):
    """
    LP direction:
      maximize sum(g) subject to g>=0 and convolve(f,g) <= M.
    This is a larger jump than the water-filling direction.
    """
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=float)
    n = f.size
    if n <= 0 or n > 1600:
        return None

    M = float(M)
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return None

    try:
        A = build_conv_matrix(f)
        c = -np.ones(n, dtype=float)
        b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, M * (1.0 + 1e-8), dtype=float)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        options = {"time_limit": max(1.0, float(time_limit)), "output_flag": False}
        res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method="highs", options=options)
        if res is not None and getattr(res, "success", False):
            x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
            if x.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
                return np.clip(x, 0.0, None)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def lp_polish(x0, deadline, max_calls=30, label=""):
    """LP-dir + segment line-search polish."""
    x = normalize_sum(np.asarray(x0, dtype=float).copy(), 1.0)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0 or n > 1600:
        return x, score_fast(x)

    N = get_fft_len(n)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    best_x = x.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(x)
    no_fail = 0
    calls = 0

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline) and calls < max_calls:
        calls += 1
        Fx = np.fft.rfft(x, N)
        A = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Fx, N)[:L].real
        maxA = float(np.max(A))
        s_cur = 2.0 * float(n) * maxA

        if np.isfinite(s_cur) and s_cur < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = s_cur
            best_x = x.copy()

        rem = max(1.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic())
        tl = min(8.0, max(1.5, rem / 4.0))
        g = solve_lp_direction(x, maxA, time_limit=tl)
        if g is None:
            no_fail += 1
            if no_fail > 3:
                break
            continue

        g = normalize_sum(g, 1.0)
        if g.size != n:
            break
        if float(np.linalg.norm(g - x)) < 1e-7:
            break

        Fg = np.fft.rfft(g, N)
        B = np.fft.irfft(Fg * Fg, N)[:L].real
        C = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Fg, N)[:L].real

        a_star, s_star = line_search_segment(A, B, C, n, best_score)
        if a_star > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s_star) and s_star < best_score - 1e-14:
            x = (1.0 - a_star) * x + a_star * g
            x = normalize_sum(x, 1.0)
            best_score = s_star
            best_x = x.copy()
            no_fail = 0
        else:
            x = best_x.copy()
            no_fail += 1
            if no_fail > 4:
                break

    return best_x, best_score


def propose_candidate(budget_s=1500.0):
    """Search for high-quality coefficient sequence."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(2024)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    result_pool = []
    pool_idx = 0

    # Load parent construction, if present.
    parent = None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
                parent = normalize_sum(p, 1.0)
                sc = score_fast(parent)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    best_score = sc
                    best_seq = parent.copy()
                    result_pool.append((sc, pool_idx, parent.copy()))
                    pool_idx += 1
                    print("Parent n=%d internal score=%.8f" % (parent.size, sc))
    except Exception as e:
        print("Could not load parent: %s" % e)

    # Candidate lengths: parent length, then 1024 and 2048.
    lengths_prio = []
    if parent is not None:
        if 256 <= parent.size <= 2048:
            lengths_prio.append(int(parent.size))
    for n in (1024, 2048):
        if n not in lengths_prio:
            lengths_prio.append(n)
    lengths = lengths_prio[:3]
    if not lengths:
        lengths = [1024]

    print("Trying lengths:", lengths)

    # Generate and score diverse initializations.
    candidates = []
    cand_idx = 0
    for n in lengths:
        inits = generate_initializations(n, parent, rng)
        for label, seq in inits:
            if seq.size != n:
                continue
            seq = normalize_sum(seq, 1.0)
            sc = score_fast(seq)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                candidates.append((sc, cand_idx, seq, n, label))
                cand_idx += 1
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_seq = seq.copy()
                if sc < 1.62:
                    result_pool.append((sc, pool_idx, seq.copy()))
                    pool_idx += 1

    candidates.sort()
    print("Best initial internal score: %.8f" % (best_score if np.isfinite(best_score) else float("inf")))

    # Quick multi-start optimization of a few promising initializations.
    quick_const_total = min(180.0, max(45.0, (deadline - t0) * 0.12))
    quick_end = t0 + quick_const_total
    top_quick = []
    quick_counts = {}
    for sc, idx, seq, n, label in candidates:
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            continue
        if quick_counts.get(n, 0) >= 2:
            continue
        top_quick.append((seq, label, n))
        quick_counts[n] = quick_counts.get(n, 0) + 1
        if len(top_quick) >= 6:
            break

    for i, (seq, label, n) in enumerate(top_quick):
        if time.monotonic() >= quick_end - 3.0:
            break
        end = min(quick_end, time.monotonic() + 28.0)
        if end <= time.monotonic() + 3.0:
            break

        seq_opt, sc_opt = qo_optimize_main(seq, end, label="quick_%s_n%d" % (label, n), rng=rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
            pool_idx += 1
            if sc_opt < best_score:
                best_score = sc_opt
                best_seq = seq_opt.copy()
        print("Quick n=%d %s: %.8f" % (n, label, sc_opt))

    # Deep optimize the current best.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 60.0 and best_seq is not None:
        dur = min(remaining * 0.34, 650.0)
        end = time.monotonic() + dur
        seq_opt, sc_opt = qo_optimize_main(best_seq, end, label="deep_main", rng=rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
            pool_idx += 1
            if sc_opt < best_score:
                best_score = sc_opt
                best_seq = seq_opt.copy()
        print("Deep main best: n=%d score=%.8f" % (best_seq.size if best_seq is not None else 0, best_score))

    # Peak-focused polish on best.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 45.0 and best_seq is not None:
        dur = min(remaining * 0.13, 300.0)
        end = time.monotonic() + dur
        seq_opt, sc_opt = qo_optimize_peak(best_seq, end, label="deep_peak", rng=rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
            pool_idx += 1
            if sc_opt < best_score:
                best_score = sc_opt
                best_seq = seq_opt.copy()
        print("Peak polish best: n=%d score=%.8f" % (best_seq.size, best_score))

    # Explore a secondary basin at the best length.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 45.0 and best_seq is not None:
        main_n = int(best_seq.size)
        dec = sqrt_flat_sequence(main_n)
        inc = dec[::-1].copy()
        second = normalize_sum(0.50 * dec + 0.50 * inc, 1.0)
        s2 = score_fast(second)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score + 0.25:
            dur = min(remaining * 0.12, 280.0)
            end = time.monotonic() + dur
            seq_opt, sc_opt = qo_optimize_main(second, end, label="deep_second", rng=rng)
            if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
                result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
                pool_idx += 1
                if sc_opt < best_score:
                    best_score = sc_opt
                    best_seq = seq_opt.copy()
            print("Second basin best: n=%d score=%.8f" % (main_n, best_score))

    # If the best is not long enough, try length 2048.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 50.0 and best_seq is not None and best_seq.size != 2048:
        n2 = 2048
        if parent is not None:
            seq2 = normalize_sum(interp_to_length(parent, n2), 1.0)
        else:
            seq2 = sqrt_flat_sequence(n2)
        dur = min(remaining * 0.11, 260.0)
        end = time.monotonic() + dur
        seq_opt, sc_opt = qo_optimize_main(seq2, end, label="deep_2048", rng=rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
            pool_idx += 1
            if sc_opt < best_score:
                best_score = sc_opt
                best_seq = seq_opt.copy()
        print("Length-2048 try best: n=%d score=%.8f" % (best_seq.size, best_score))

    # Final fine polish.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 35.0 and best_seq is not None:
        dur = min(remaining * 0.28, 220.0)
        end = time.monotonic() + dur
        seq_opt, sc_opt = qo_optimize_fine(best_seq, end, label="fine", rng=rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
            pool_idx += 1
            if sc_opt < best_score:
                best_score = sc_opt
                best_seq = seq_opt.copy()
        print("Fine polish best: n=%d score=%.8f" % (best_seq.size, best_score))

    # Optional LP polish.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 35.0 and best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 1600:
        dur = min(remaining * 0.25, 180.0)
        end = time.monotonic() + dur
        seq_opt, sc_opt = lp_polish(best_seq, end, max_calls=25, label="lp")
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
            pool_idx += 1
            if sc_opt < best_score:
                best_score = sc_opt
                best_seq = seq_opt.copy()
        print("LP polish best: n=%d score=%.8f" % (best_seq.size, best_score))

    # One last short main run if time remains.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 25.0 and best_seq is not None:
        end = time.monotonic() + min(remaining * 0.65, 120.0)
        seq_opt, sc_opt = qo_optimize_main(best_seq, end, label="final_main", rng=rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            result_pool.append((sc_opt, pool_idx, seq_opt.copy()))
            pool_idx += 1
            if sc_opt < best_score:
                best_score = sc_opt
                best_seq = seq_opt.copy()

    # Always add final best.
    if best_seq is not None and np.isfinite(score_fast(best_seq)):
        result_pool.append((best_score, pool_idx, best_seq.copy()))
        pool_idx += 1

    # Choose the best official score among the stored pool.
    if result_pool:
        result_pool.sort()
        final_seq = None
        final_off = float("inf")
        eval_top = 8
        for sc, _, seq in result_pool[:eval_top]:
            if seq is None:
                continue
            try:
                off = evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in seq])
            except Exception:
                off = float("inf")
            if np.isfinite(off) and off < final_off:
                final_off = off
                final_seq = seq.copy()

        if final_seq is not None:
            best_seq = final_seq
            print("FINAL chosen official=%.8f internal_pool=%.8f n=%d" % (final_off, best_score, best_seq.size))
            return [float(v) for v in best_seq]

    # Fallback.
    fallback = normalize_sum(np.ones(512, dtype=float), 1.0)
    try:
        evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in fallback])
    except Exception:
        pass
    print("Using fallback uniform 512.")
    return [float(v) for v in fallback]
```
